❯ what is it                                                                    

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Pinprick is a first-person exploration game you made on Oct 9, in the style of
  Scanner Sombre. It's a single HTML file that runs in the browser.

  - The idea: You're in a pitch-black underground level. Your scanner sprays
    dots of light onto whatever it hits, and the dots fade over time. You never
    see walls directly, only the dots, so the picture builds up as you scan.
  - The goal: Collect three signal cores. When you have all three, an amber door
    near the start opens and you walk out.
  - Controls on the phone: Drag anywhere to get a virtual joystick for walking,
    and drag to aim. Hold the SCAN button to paint dots, and tap SWEEP for one
    dense top-to-bottom pass, which then takes 30 seconds to recharge.
  - Controls on a computer: WASD to walk, the mouse to look, and hold click or
    Space to scan.
  - Finding your way: A hot/cold meter and a quiet ping that pans left or right
    point you to the nearest core, then to the exit. The game recommends playing
    with sound.

  There are no enemies or timer, so it's a calm exploration game rather than
  horror.

✻ Crunched for 22s · done 8:14 PM

❯ create a plan for what the next steps or additions sre to male it more of a   
  gorror game and more of a playable gsme and what to improve fix or implement  

  Read 1 file, ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Pinprick plan: from calm exploration to horror

  While reading the code I found a bug that stops anyone finishing the game, so
  that comes first.

  The game can't be won. The amber exit pad counts as a solid object, so you
  stop 1.0 m from its centre. The win check needs you within 0.95 m. I walked
  into the pad from four sides in a test, and 1.00 m is as close as you get. The
  fix is one line: let the player walk onto the pad.

  Why it isn't scary yet

  - Nothing can hurt you. There's no enemy, no way to die and no timer.
  - You can't get lost. The signal bars and ping lead you straight to every
    core, and getting lost is where fear starts.
  - The scanner never runs out. You can hold it forever, so you never have to
    stand in the dark.
  - The ambient sound is calm. It's two soft notes a fifth apart.
  - It's short and the same every time. There's one small map, about 3 minutes
    long, with fixed core spots.

  Phase 1: Fixes and groundwork

  1. Fix the exit so the game can be won.
  2. Add a pause menu: a button on the phone, Esc on a computer. Right now Esc
     only frees the mouse.
  3. Add settings for look speed, invert look, volume, and swapping the stick
     and scan sides.
  4. Save your best time and death count on the phone.
  5. Move the level out of the code into a separate level format, so new levels
     are easy to add.
  6. Speed up scanning before the map gets bigger. Each scanner ray currently
     checks every object in the level, which will lag on a larger map.

  Phase 2: Something hunting you (the core of the horror)

  - The rule: seeing makes you visible. The scanner hum, footsteps and
    especially the sweep make noise, and a creature hunts by that noise.
  - The creature looks like everything else. Dots land on it like any surface,
    so it looks like part of the room. You notice it because it moves.
  - The scariest part comes for free. Dots stay where they landed for 20
    seconds. If you scan it and look again later, you see the old outline where
    it stood and the new one, closer.
  - Getting caught: it kills you, and you restart at the last core you picked
    up.
  - Escape options: crouch to go quiet, and hide in lockers or behind crates.
  - The finale: after the third core it stops creeping and chases you to the
    exit.

  Phase 3: Make the scanner a risk

  - Overheating: holding scan heats it up, and when it overheats you're forced
    into the dark until it cools.
  - A trade-off for the sweep: it shows you the whole room but tells the
    creature exactly where you are.
  - Unreliable signal bars: they read only while you stand still, and fill with
    static when the creature is near. That makes them a danger warning too.

  Phase 4: Atmosphere and story

  - Sound:
    - Swap the calm ambient notes for dissonant ones.
    - Add distant metal creaks and dripping.
    - Add its footsteps panned left or right, and breathing when it's close.
    - Leave silence before a scare.
  - Writing only the scanner can see, in a different dot colour, e.g. "IT HEARS
    THE SCANNER".
  - Scripted scares:
    - A figure appears right behind you when you turn round.
    - The statue in the north gallery has moved the next time you scan it.
    - A door slams shut behind you.
  - Story: notes from the last scanner operator, whose old dots are still
    faintly on the walls.
  - Phone vibration when it's close or when you get caught.

  Phase 5: More game

  - Make 3–5 levels that get harder, with the first one quietly teaching scan
    and sweep.
  - Shuffle where the cores are on each run.
  - Add difficulty modes, e.g. easy keeps the signal bars working normally.
  - Add sprinting, which is faster but louder.
  - Expand the end screen: time, dots thrown, deaths and best time.
  - Make it installable as a phone app so it opens from the home screen and
    works offline. Right now the fonts load from the internet.

  Suggested order: Phase 1, then Phase 2 with a basic creature, then try it on
  the phone. The creature is what makes it horror; everything after builds on
  it.

  Want me to start with the exit fix and the creature, then republish to the
  same link?

      31 +  function plot(data, i, T, life, gain, H, W, k0, vp) {        
      32 +    const o = i * 8, x = data[o], y = data[o + 1], z = data[o +
         + 2], age = T - data[o + 3];                                    
      33 +    if (age < 0 || age > life) return;                         
      34 +    const w = vp[3] * x + vp[7] * y + vp[11] * z + vp[15];     
      35 +    if (w < 0.05) return;                                      
      36 +    const sx = ((vp[0] * x + vp[4] * y + vp[8] * z + vp[12]) / 
         +w + 1) * W / 2;                                                
      37 +    const sy = (1 - (vp[1] * x + vp[5] * y + vp[9] * z + vp[13]
         +) / w) * H / 2;                                                
      38 +    if (sx < -10 || sy < -10 || sx > W + 10 || sy > H + 10) ret
         +urn;                                                           
      39 +    const k = 1 - age / life, fade = k * (2 - k), flash = Math.
         +exp(-age * 6) * 0.55;                                          
      40 +    const sz = Math.min(13, Math.max(1.6, k0 * data[o + 7] / w)
         +);

         +h.min(255, (data[o + 6] + flash) * m) | 0})`;

● Pinprick has been rebuilt as a horror game with four levels, a creature that
  hunts by sound, and a proper project structure.

  Where to play it:
  - Online: ⧉ https://claude.ai/artifact/XADeZVR9fUhVRrzJvuueWh (same link as
    before, updated).
  - On your phone: /sdcard/Download/pinprick.html is the new version. The
    original is still there as pinprick-v1.html.
  - As an app: tap pinprick.apk in Download (My Files) to install it. It runs
    full screen, works offline, keeps the screen awake, and the back button
    pauses the game.

  What's in it now
  - Fixed: the exit bug, so levels can be finished.
  - Four levels:
    - Intake: a tutorial with nothing hunting you.
    - Sublevel: your original map, with a new loop and a crawlspace. The
      creature wakes when you take the first core.
    - Cistern: the creature is already awake, and a gate slams behind you.
    - Lift Shaft: four cores, then a chase to the lift.
  - The creature: it can't see, so it hunts by noise from your footsteps, the
    scanner hum and the sweep. Sound reaches it round corners, not through
    walls. Its old dots stay behind when it moves, so you see where it was. If
    it catches you, you restart at your last core.
  - Staying alive:
    - Hold the scanner for about 9 seconds and it overheats and cuts out.
    - Sprinting is fast but loud and drains your breath.
    - Crouching is quiet and fits through crawlspaces the creature can't follow
      you into.
    - Crouching still in a dark corner hides you.
    - The signal bars only read while you stand still, and they turn to static
      when it's close.
  - Atmosphere: a dissonant drone, distant creaks, its footsteps panned left or
    right, breathing, a heartbeat and red screen edges as it gets close. There's
    also phone vibration, red wall writing that only shows in dots, notes from
    the last operator, and their old rust-coloured dots.
  - Scares: a figure behind you, a statue that moves when you're not looking,
    slamming doors, and whispers.
  - Menus:
    - Pause (with a pause button on the phone).
    - Settings: look speed, invert look, volume, left-handed controls,
      vibration, battery saver.
    - Easy, Normal and Hard.
    - Levels unlock in order, and best times are saved.

  How it's built
  - Structure: the project lives in ~/projects/pinprick, split into about 30
    small files (world, game rules, rendering, sound, controls, one file per
    level). The build step turns them into the single 143 KB HTML file. I chose
    plain JavaScript with no game engine because your custom renderer is already
    the right tool, and an engine would add around 600 KB without helping.
  - Performance:
    - Each scanner ray now only checks objects near its path instead of every
      object in the level.
    - The game only draws dots that are still visible.
    - Screen updates are skipped when nothing has changed.
  - Commands: ./build.sh runs the tests and builds the game. ./build.sh apk also
    builds the app. To add a level, see the README.

  Testing
  - Automated tests: all 29 pass. They check that every core, note and exit in
    every level can be reached, including with doors slammed shut. A bot then
    finishes all four levels using the real game code. The tests also check the
    creature catches a noisy player but not a hidden one.
  - Headless browser run: a browser run with no screen played through the
    screens on desktop and phone layouts with no errors. It ran at about 60 fps
    on the phone layout.
  - Not checked: the headless browser on your phone crashes as soon as it tries
    to draw the dots, even on a ten-line test page. I checked the visuals with a
    stand-in that redraws the dots, so how the real dots look will only show
    when you play it on your phone. I also couldn't listen to the sound or feel
    the vibration.
  - Tuning: heat time, creature speed and noise ranges are all in src/config.js
    if anything feels too easy or too hard.

  The project folder is a git repository, but I haven't committed anything.

✻ Cogitated for 25s · done 9:07 PM

※ recap: Pinprick is now a four-level horror game with a creature that hunts by sound, published to the same link and saved to your Download folder as the HTML and an app. Next, play it on your phone, since that's the only place the dots render. (disable recaps in /config)

● Artifact("dist/artifact.html")
  ⎿  Updated ⧉ https://claude.ai/artifact/XADeZVR9fUhVRrzJvuueWh

● The creature is scarier, and the scanner is now a real 3D gun that fires the
  dots. Both are updated everywhere:
  - Online: ⧉ https://claude.ai/artifact/XADeZVR9fUhVRrzJvuueWh (same link).
  - Phone file: /sdcard/Download/pinprick.html.
  - App: /sdcard/Download/pinprick.apk. Tap it to install it over the old one.

  What it looks like
  - Shape: it's taller now, with its head pushed far forward, a hanging jaw, a
    lump on its skull, and long fingers that nearly touch the floor. Its head
    jerks to new angles instead of moving smoothly.
  - Hunting: it drops onto all fours and scuttles at you, with its head low.
  - On the scanner: its dots are a faint bone-white, so you get that
    "something's wrong" moment when you spot it.

  What it sounds like
  - Its voice: a distorted, warbling throat that almost sounds human. It groans
    to itself while it wanders, and shrieks when it finds you.
  - Other sounds:
    - Wet echolocation clicks that speed up.
    - A dragging foot when it walks upright.
    - A four-beat gallop when it's on all fours.
    - A rattling breath.
  - Mimicry: once it has heard you, it copies sounds you've made from where it
    stands, slightly wrong. That includes your scanner click, the signal ping,
    the core chime played backwards, and your footsteps.
  - Getting close: the tunnels now echo, and its sounds get duller through rock.
    A low rumble you feel more than hear builds as it gets closer, the
    background noise drops away, and your heartbeat speeds up.

  How encounters play out
  - First sighting: the first time your scanner lands on it, you get a dissonant
    musical stab and your heart jumps.
  - Holding your beam on it: it freezes, turns its head to you, then shrieks and
    charges. The last few metres are a lunge.
  - Hiding: if you're hiding as it passes, it may stop right beside you,
    clicking and breathing, and "Don't move." appears on screen. Stand up or
    move and it has you.
  - Ambushes: sometimes it just stands silent and still in the dark.
  - When it's close: your scanner's dots scatter and some come back red.
  - Getting caught: your view is wrenched round to its face, it rears up with
    its arms spread and shrieks in your face, and the scanner floods it red.
    Then everything goes black and your ears ring.

  The gun
  - Look: a gunmetal scanner pistol with a teal emitter, and coils that glow as
    it charges. Cooling fins go orange to red as it heats, and a screen on the
    back shows charge, heat and sweep.
  - Firing: every dot now comes out of the emitter tip and converges on your
    crosshair. Because the light comes from the gun, the creature casts a dark
    shadow on the wall behind it.
  - Handling:
    - It sways and bobs as you walk, lags behind fast turns and tilts when you
      strafe.
    - It lowers when you sprint and trembles while charging.
    - The sweep kicks it back, and it dips and shakes when it overheats.
    - It pulls back near walls and falls away when you're caught.
  - Screen fit: it sits in the same place on any screen. On a phone held upright
    it moves inward so the SCAN button doesn't cover it.

  Testing
  - Automated tests: all 32 pass, including new ones for the beam making it
    charge, it lingering beside a hidden player, and the timing of the death
    sequence. A bot still finishes every level.
  - Not checked: the headless browser on your phone crashes on any drawing at
    all, not just dots. My screenshots show the gun's real shape, position and
    lighting maths redrawn in software. I haven't seen the real GPU output,
    heard any of the new sound, or felt the vibration. Your phone is the first
    real test, so headphones on.

  If the beam triggers it too easily, or the gun sits too big or too low, those
  are single numbers in src/config.js and at the top of src/game/viewmodel.js.

● The APK was there, but Android hadn't added it to its file index, so it didn't
  show in some file-manager views. I've saved a fresh copy under a new name,
  which Android picks up as a new file, and deleted the old copy.

  To install:
  1. Open My Files, then Internal storage, then Download. Go to the folder
     itself; the "Downloads" shortcut may not show it.
  2. Look for pinprick-game.apk. Your Download folder has about 60 other APKs,
     so search "pinprick" or sort by date with newest first.
  3. Tap it and choose Install. If Android asks, allow My Files to install apps.

  If it still doesn't appear, tell me which file manager you're using.

  squeezing the pattern and leaving screen edges uncovered, with no visible
  beam linking it to the gun. I'll fix it by having rays target the correct

● Artifact("dist/artifact.html")
  ⎿  Updated ⧉ https://claude.ai/artifact/XADeZVR9fUhVRrzJvuueWh

● The sweep now fires from the gun and covers the whole screen.

  What was wrong: when I made the gun fire the dots, the sweep's rays started at
  the gun tip, about 0.7 m in front of your eyes, but still pointed the way
  your eyes look. From where you stand, that squeezed the picture toward the
  middle and left the edges dark. The sweep was also still shown as a flat line
  sliding down the screen, so it didn't look like the gun was doing it.

  What changed:
  - Full coverage: each sweep ray first finds the spot you'd see on screen, then
    the gun fires at that spot. In my test the whole view now gets painted, in
    both landscape and portrait, from three different places in the level.
  - It comes from the gun: the flat screen line is gone. A translucent sheet of
    light now fans out from the emitter to the line it's painting, and moves
    down the room.
  - The gun follows it: the barrel tilts from up to down as it sweeps, then
    settles back.
  - Shadows: if something is between the gun and a spot you can see, the light
    lands on that object instead, which gives a small shadow.

  I added a test that fails if a sweep ever misses part of the screen again. All
  35 tests pass.

  Get it:
  - App: in My Files, open Internal storage → Download and install
    pinprick-game-2.apk. I saved it under a new name so it shows up in the file
    manager, and deleted the old APK.
  - Phone file: /sdcard/Download/pinprick.html is updated.
  - Online: ⧉ https://claude.ai/artifact/XADeZVR9fUhVRrzJvuueWh
